## **Avaleha** 

### **General Description:** 

Avaleha or Lehya is a semi-solid preparation of drugs, prepared with addition of jaggery, sugar or sugar-candy and boiled with prescribed juices or decoction. These preparations generally have 

- Kwatha  or other liquids 

- Jaggery, sugar or sugar-candy 

- Powders or pulps of certain drugs 

- Ghee or oil and Honey 

### **General Method of preparation** 

Jaggery, sugar or sugar-candy is dissolved in the liquid and strained to remove the foreign particles. This solution is boiled over a moderate fire. When pressed between two fingers if Paka becomes thready (Tantuvat), or when it sinks in water without getting easily dissolved, it should be removed from the fire. Fine powders of drugs are then added in small quantities and stirred continuously to form a homogenous mixture. Ghee or oil, if mentioned, is added while the preparation is still hot and mixed well. Honey, if mentioned is added when the preparation becomes cool and mixed well. The Lehya should neither be hard nor a thick fluid. When pulp of the drugs is added and ghee or oil is present in the preparation, this can be rolled between the fingers. When metals are mentioned, the Bhasmas of the metals are used. In case of drugs like Bhall¡taka, purification process is to be followed. The Lehya should be kept in glass or porcelain jars. It can also be kept in a metal container which does not react with it. Normally, Lehyas should be used within one year.

## **Ghrita** 

### **General Description:** 

Ghritas are preparations in which the Ghrita is boiled with prescribed liquid media [Svarasa / Kashaya etc.] and a fine paste [Kalka] of the drugs specified in the formulation composition. Unless specified otherwise Ghrita means Go Ghrita. 

### **General Method of Preparation:** 

There are usually three essential components in the manufacture of Ghrita Kalpana- Drava [Any liquid medium as prescribed in the composition], Kalka [Fine paste of the specified drugs], Sneha dravya [Ghrita] And, occasionally Gandha dravya [Perfuming agents] 

If the kalka is one part by weight , ghrita should be 4parts and 16 parts of water are added in the place of drava dravya 

If drava dravya is kwath, 1part kalka and 6 part ghrita is taken. 

If the drava dravya is swarasa, milk, butter milk, dadhi etc, 1part kalka, 8 part ghrita is taken, 16 times water is also added to ensure dissolution of active ingredients of drugs. 

### **Three stages of Paka are specified for therapeutic purposes.** 

a. Mridu Paka: In this stage, the Kalka looks waxy and when rolled between fingers, it rolls like lac without sticking. The Ghrita obtained at this stage is used for Nasya [Nasal instillation]. 

b. Madhyama Paka: In this stage, the Kalka becomes harder and rolls into Varti. It burns without crackling sounds when exposed to fire and phena [froth] will disappears in Ghrita. The Ghrita obtained at this stage is used for Pāna [Internal administration] and Vasti [Enema]. 

c. Khara Paka: Further heating of the Ghrita, leads to Khara pāka. Kalka becomes brittle when rolled in between fingers. The Ghrita obtained at this stage is used only for Abhyanga [External application]

|Method of preparation|Milk should be kept in a vessel. It should be heated in mild heat and<br>stirred with ladle. When milk is reduced to half or one third then, it is<br>called Lehya' when it is reduced to sixth part then it is named as<br>'Ghațika' when it is reduced to eighth part then it is called 'sharkara'.<br>The aforesaid four types of milk should be kept in seperate pots and it<br>should remain there for a long period. Fruits and flowers should be<br>dropped in all these milk. Milk which has become drinkable is to be<br>added by the fruits and flowers. In the milk, which has become more<br>solidified, the Ardraka should be added. the flower of jasmine are<br>again to be added to it. When milk becomes lickable the palm (date)<br>fruit is added. The milk in the form of nectar is also added by the<br>another fruit. Later on the flowers of Punnaga should also be added to<br>it. The milk known as ghatika is to be added by the mango fruit along<br>with ghee and honey, In this way, the pomegranate flower and rice<br>should be added, when the milk becomes in 'Sharkara' form, banana<br>fruit is to be added along with sugar. It removes the unsensitiveness<br>of tongue. Afterwards Punnaga flower and ketaki fruit should also be<br>added. The cook should add some appropriate flower in drinkable<br>milk and lastly all the flowers should be kept out from the milk and<br>milk should be kept inpugpatta and it is to be served in meals.|
|---|---|
|Best use before|3 years|
|Properties/Health<br>benefits|Madhura (sweet in taste), guru (heavy to digest) , sheeta (cold in<br>potency),nidrakara(induces sleep),snigdha(unctuous)|
|Classical Indications|Sarvadoshahara<br>(Alleviates<br>all<br>the<br>three<br>doshas),<br>Vrishya<br>(Aphrodisiac)|
|Contraindications if any|Madhumeha(diabetes)|
|Specialprecautions|-|
|Others|Anupana -Normalwater/lukewarmwater|

## **Mantha** 

Common preparation method of mantha- 

Grind all the ingredients into a paste and soak them in four times the amount of water for 2-3 hours. Subsequently, churn the mixture thoroughly and filter it.

|Method of preparation|Fill three-fourths of the cauldron (Sthali) with water and place it on<br>the stove or chulha. Once the water is heated, add well-washed rice,<br>equivalent to one-fourth of the vessel. When the Shali rice is semi-<br>cooked, mix either completely cooked or semi-cooked meat, cut into<br>small pieces, along with salt. Cook the rice with clarified ghee until<br>the watery residue disappears. Place it on the coal fire (Angara). Mix<br>coconut water (Narikela jala) and ghrita, and scent it with the flower<br>of screw pine (Ketaki). Add pieces of Parpata and make it fragrant<br>with a mixture of Karpura and Musk (Kasturi). One should cover the<br>mouth of the cauldron and seal the lid holes with kneaded flour to<br>preserve the scented smell. Rotate it on the coal fire until the mixture<br>becomes soft.|
|Best use before|Within a day|
|Properties/Health<br>benefits|Tikta (bitter), katu (pungent), madhura (sweet in taste), ruchikaraka<br>(imparts taste), viryavardhaka (aphrodisiac), laghu (light for<br>digestion)|
|Classical Indications|Vrishya<br>(Aphrodisiac),<br>Balpradama<br>(Promotes<br>strength),<br>Dhatuvriddhikar (Promotes the growth and development of dhatus),<br>Vranadoshaprashamana (Woundhealing).|

Method of preparation Collect the tender, fresh, and semi-ripe fruit of bitter gourd. Cut off the stem part of the fruit and place it in a cauldron, mixing it with Kanji water (sour water) to eliminate bitterness. Transfer this kanji-dipped bitter gourd fruit to another cauldron, mixing it with saline water, and cook it. then fry it with ghee (clarified butter), and one part of the fruit should be punctured and cut into coin-shaped slices using a rod. Safely keep the sliced pieces aside. Cut and remove the seeds of bitter gourd, making them into vatika. <u>Cut the seed part of the bitter gourd into the</u> 

||finest pieces with a sharp knife. In the whole mixture, add milk, soured<br>small ball-sized chunks or bari, pieces of coconut, Lashuna, and<br>Ardraka. Mix in the powder of Maricha, Dhanyaka, spices, salt, karpura,<br>etc., along with ghee. Prepare it in pulp form and place it in a greased<br>pot. Fill up the bitter gourd with this pulp, close it with a lid kept for this<br>purpose,tie the bittergourdwith threads,andput it into hotghee.|
|---|---|
|Best use before|Within a day|
|Properties/Health<br>benefits|Katu (pungent), tikta (bitter), laghu (light for digestion), ushna (hot in<br>potency),snigdha(unctuous)|
|Classical Indications|Kaphahara (Alleviates kapha dosha), Vatahara (Alleviates vata dosha),<br>Sarvarogahara (Mitigates all the diseases), Jwara (Fever), Agnimandhya<br>(Loss of appetite)|

The boiled<br>milk, mixed with the juice of jackfruit (panasa) and screw pine (ketaki),<br>should be dropped into another pot. Another pot should contain the juice<br>of sugarcane and lotus flower. In a separate pot, the juice of mango fruit<br>with pulp, along with other aromatic flowers like champaka, should be<br>dropped. In another pot, the juice of grapes mixed with the juice of lotus<br>flower should be kept. use the juice of pomegranate fruit, making it<br>fragrant with Mallika flowers, and adding saffron. Similarly, in another<br>pot, the juice of wooden apple fruit and the flower of cumin seeds (ajaji)<br>should be dropped.|

## **Peya** 

Common preparation method of peya kalpana - 

Prepare this mixture using rice (tandula) and water in a 1:14 ratio. Boil the rice with 14 parts of water in a vessel over a gentle fire until it is properly cooked or softened. Collect the cooked rice along with the supernatant water in a separate vessel.

## **Vilepi** 

Common preparation method of Vilepi :- 

To prepare this Kalpana, mix Tandula (rice) and water in a 1:4 ratio. Take Tandula (rice) in a vessel along with four parts of water and boil it over a gentle flame until the rice is properly cooked or softened.

## **Yavagu** 

Common preparation method of yavagu :- 

Take all the herbs in specified quantity and prepare a paste. Boil the herbal paste in 16 parts of water until 8 parts of water/decoction remains. Strain the decoction through a cotton cloth into a vessel. Add rice to the decoction in a ratio of 1:6. Cook over medium flame until the rice is properly cooked/softened.

## **Yusha** 

Common preparation method of Yusha Kalpana. – 

Prepare Yusha by cooking pulses/legumes in 16 parts of water. Mix 1 part of Mudga and all ingredients in a vessel, adding 16 parts of water. Cook the mixture over medium flame until it reaches a thick consistency.
